--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission analysis:** Robots need to reach the BLUE-walled strip (floor is BLACK). They start in the center (GRAY floor). The score increases for every robot outside the target region, so we want robots to find and stay in the BLACK-floor strip near BLUE walls.

**Key perceptions:**
- Floor BLACK = robot is in one of the two strips (could be target or non-target)
- Floor GRAY = robot is in the middle, not in target
- Blue walls = target strip (colour 3 in ARGoS)
- Green walls = wrong strip (colour 2)

**Champion analysis:** The champion uses repulsion + spiral with transitions based on random probability and gray floor detection. Score 17264 is decent but can improve. The champion seems to be doing general exploration without specifically targeting blue walls.

**Better strategy:**
1. Use GoToColor with blue (clr=3) to move toward blue walls
2. When on BLACK floor, stop or stay (could be in target zone)
3. When seeing blue walls, keep moving toward them
4. Avoid green walls (GoAwayColor with green=2)

**State designs:**
- State with GoToColor blue (B=8, clr=3) as primary driver
- Transition on BLACK floor with high probability (likely in target zone)
- Stop or slow movement when on black floor
- Escape from gray with exploration

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion (exact copy)** - survival
2. **Champion variant** - small tweaks to probabilities
3. **GoToColor blue** - direct approach to blue walls
4. **GoAwayColor green** - avoid green walls
5. **3-state: explore → GoToColor blue → stop on black**
6. **2-state: exploration → GoToColor blue**
7. **2-state: GoToColor blue → stop on black**
8. **3-state with neighbor conditions**
9. **Zigzag + GoToColor blue**
10. **FlockCenter + GoToColor blue**
11. **4-state comprehensive**
12. **GoToColor blue with multiple transitions**
13. **Exploration with black floor detection**
14. **Mixed approach**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Champion analysis:** Score jumped from 17264 to 3333! The champion is:
- State 0: GoAwayColor GREEN (clr=2), transitions to state 1 when seeing BLUE (colour 3) with p=0.8, OR when on BLACK floor with p=0.8
- State 1: GoToColor BLUE (clr=3), transitions back to state 0 when on GRAY floor with p=0.6

This makes excellent sense: robots flee green walls, and when they see blue or hit black floor they switch to actively approaching blue. When they land on gray (middle), they go back to fleeing green.

**Strategy:** The champion is already very good. I should:
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Exploit: tweak probabilities (0.8→0.85, 0.6→0.7, etc.)
3. Explore: try adding more transitions, different state counts, stopping on black floor, using neighbor conditions

Key insight: When on BLACK floor near BLUE walls, maybe staying still is better than moving. Also, could add a 3rd state that stops when on black floor.

**Variants to try:**
- Higher/lower transition probabilities
- Add stop state when on black floor
- Add exploration state
- Try different combinations of conditions
- Try 3-state versions with stop on black

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion hasn't improved from round 2 to 3 (still 3333.66). The champion logic is:
- State 0: GoAwayColor GREEN (flee green walls), transitions to state 1 when seeing BLUE (c=7,l=3,p=0.8) OR on BLACK floor (c=0,p=0.8)
- State 1: GoToColor BLUE (approach blue walls), transitions back to state 0 when on GRAY floor (c=1,p=0.6)

This is a strong design. The champion score of 3333 means robots spend significant time outside. Let me think about what could improve it:

1. When robots reach the black floor (target), they should STOP or stay - currently state 1 (GoToColor blue) might overshoot
2. The transition from state 1 back to state 0 on GRAY could be problematic - maybe lower p so they stay in state 1 longer
3. Adding a STOP state when on black floor could help robots stay in target zone
4. The p=0.8 on black floor transition might cause robots in the WRONG black strip to also switch to GoToColor blue - but then they'd approach blue walls correctly anyway

Key issue: Both strips have BLACK floor. Robots in the green strip (wrong) also see black floor and transition to GoToColor blue - which is correct behavior! So the logic seems sound.

Let me try:
- Reducing the gray-floor transition probability (keep robots in GoToColor state longer)
- Adding stop state on black floor
- Trying different probability combinations
- 3-state versions with explicit stop

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion is stuck at 3333.66 for multiple rounds. The champion topology works well but we need to break out of this local optimum. Let me think more carefully.

**Champion logic recap:**
- State 0: GoAwayColor GREEN (flee green walls), go to state 1 if see BLUE (p=0.8) OR on BLACK floor (p=0.8)
- State 1: GoToColor BLUE (approach blue walls), go back to state 0 if on GRAY (p=0.6)

**Why score is still 3333?** With 20 robots over 1200 steps, perfect score would be 0. 3333 means on average ~2.8 robots are outside target per step. The main issue might be:
1. Robots oscillate between states too much
2. Robots in target zone (black floor, blue walls) still transition back to state 0 sometimes when they see gray floor edges
3. Need a STOP state when clearly in target zone

**New ideas:**
- Add a 3rd state: STOP when on black floor AND seeing blue walls (both conditions)
- Try having state 1 transition to stop on black floor, only return on gray
- Try single-state: just GoToColor blue (simple but might work)
- The issue with `--c0x1 9` error - condition 9 doesn't exist, valid are 0,1,2,3,4,5,7
- Note: I should NOT use `--cle0 3` (invalid LED color)

Let me try more diverse topologies this round since exploitation hasn't helped:

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion is stuck at 3333.66 for many rounds. My exploitation attempts haven't improved it. I need to think more carefully about what's happening and try genuinely different approaches.

**Deep analysis of the problem:**
- Target: BLACK floor strip with BLUE walls
- Wrong strip: BLACK floor with GREEN walls  
- Middle: GRAY floor
- Score: 1 per robot per step outside target

**Champion logic works but isn't perfect.** The issue is that when robots reach the BLACK floor (could be either strip), they transition to GoToColor BLUE. If they're in the WRONG (green) strip, they correctly approach blue. But if they're in the RIGHT (blue) strip already, GoToColor blue keeps them moving toward the wall which is fine, but they might overshoot or bounce.

**Key insight I've been missing:** When a robot IS in the target zone (black floor + sees blue walls), it should STOP. The champion doesn't have a stop state. Let me build FSMs where:
1. When on BLACK floor AND seeing BLUE → STOP
2. When on GRAY → explore/flee green

Also, I notice the `--n1x0 0` in a 1-state machine was wrong (state 0 can't transition to itself). Let me fix that - with 1 state there's only 1 state so no valid transitions. Actually with 1 state `--n0 0` transitions should work... but destination must be a different state. 1-state machines can't have transitions. Let me avoid them.

**New topology ideas:**
- Stop as primary state, escape via gray floor detection
- GoToColor blue as only behavior (no transitions back)
- Try color 6 (cyan) for blue walls instead of 3

Wait - let me re-read the colour coding. The walls are BLUE. In ARGoS colour IDs: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. So blue walls = colour 3. That's correct in the champion.

Let me try truly different structures:

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

New champion at 3150.47! The key change: state 1 (GoToColor BLUE) now transitions back to state 0 with p=0.2 (very low) instead of p=0.6. This means once robots enter GoToColor BLUE mode, they stay there much longer, which makes sense - keep approaching the target.

**Champion analysis:**
- State 0: GoAwayColor GREEN, transitions to GoToColor BLUE when seeing blue walls (p=0.8) OR on black floor (p=0.8)
- State 1: GoToColor BLUE, transitions back to state 0 when on GRAY floor with only p=0.2

The low p=0.2 means robots rarely abandon the "go to blue" behavior. This is the key insight - once they're heading to blue, keep them going.

**Exploitation ideas:**
- Try p=0.1, p=0.05, p=0.15 for the gray transition
- Try p=0.0 effectively (never go back) - but need at least one transition
- Try very small probability like 0.05
- Add a second transition from state 1 (e.g., seeing green walls = go back to flee)
- Try 3-state with stop on black floor

**Key question:** Should the gray-floor transition from state 1 be even lower? Try p=0.1, p=0.05. Or should we remove the ability to go back at all (p→0)?

Also, the `--n1x0 0` in champion means destination 0 from state 1 (which is state 0 itself since destination indices skip current state - wait, let me re-read).

With 2 states: from state 1, others are [state 0], so D=0 means state 0. Correct.

## Reasoning

New champion at 3015.86! The trend is clear: reducing the gray-floor transition probability in state 1 improves performance. We went from p=0.6 → p=0.2 → p=0.05. 

**The pattern:** Once robots are in GoToColor BLUE mode, they should almost never leave it. The gray floor transition with very low probability just barely allows escape from rare stuck situations.

**Next logical step:** Try even lower probabilities: p=0.02, p=0.01, p=0.005, p=0.001. Also try adding NO gray transition (but must have at least 1 transition - use a tiny fixed probability instead of gray condition).

**Key explorations:**
1. Even lower p for gray transition: 0.02, 0.01, 0.005, 0.001
2. Replace gray transition with a tiny fixed probability (C=5) so robots almost never leave state 1
3. Try adding a second transition from state 1 (seeing green walls → flee)
4. Vary the state 0 transition probabilities (0.8 → higher/lower)
5. Try 3-state with stop

The champion topology is very robust. Let me focus on fine-tuning the p value for the gray transition and also explore whether removing state 0's black-floor transition helps (only use blue-wall detection to enter state 1).

## Reasoning

Excellent progress! Champion improved to 2891.47 with p=0.001 for the gray transition. The trend is clear: making the gray-floor transition probability as small as possible keeps robots in GoToColor BLUE mode almost permanently.

**The limit:** p=0.001 is very small. Can we go lower? The parameter is a real in [0..1], so we could try p=0.0001 or even p=0.0. But p=0.0 might cause issues if it's not allowed. Let me try very small values.

**Key insight:** The gray transition at p=0.001 means on average a robot stays in state 1 for 1000 steps before transitioning back. The run is 1200 steps, so once they enter state 1 they almost never leave. This is nearly equivalent to having no transition back.

**Alternative:** Replace the gray condition with a fixed probability (C=5) at p=0.001 - this fires probabilistically every step regardless of floor. Or use C=5 with even smaller p.

**Also consider:** What if we use a fixed tiny probability (C=5) instead of gray condition? This would allow robots stuck anywhere to occasionally reset.

**Exploration:**
- Try p=0.0001 for gray transition
- Try C=5 with p=0.001 (fires every step randomly, not just on gray)
- Try C=5 with p=0.0001
- Try completely removing the back-transition (but need at least 1 - use C=5 p=0.0001)
- Vary state 0 probabilities: try higher (0.9, 0.95) or lower (0.7)
- Try 3-state version with stop on black floor
- Try single-state GoToColor blue (need a self-loop workaround - can't point to own state)

## Reasoning

New champion at 2885.00! The winning change: state 0 now only has ONE transition (see BLUE walls with p=0.8), removing the black-floor transition. This makes sense - only switch to GoToColor BLUE when you actually see blue walls, not just any black floor (which could be the wrong strip).

**Champion:**
- State 0: GoAwayColor GREEN, only transition: see BLUE walls (C=7, l=3, p=0.8) → go to state 1
- State 1: GoToColor BLUE, only transition: gray floor (C=1, p=0.001) → back to state 0

This is nearly optimal: flee green until you see blue, then go to blue permanently (almost).

**Final round explorations:**
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Try p=0.0001 for gray transition (even lower)
3. Try different p for blue-wall detection (0.9, 0.95, 0.7)
4. Try replacing gray transition with fixed probability C=5 p=0.001
5. Try 3-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToBlue → Stop(on black)
6. Try state 0 with additional black floor transition (back to original but with single-state-0-transition)
7. Try GoAwayGreen with C=5 tiny probability as only transition (no condition needed)
8. What if state 0 uses C=7 blue with p=0.95?
9. What about using C=0 (black floor) as only transition from state 0?

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
